Implement the following plan:

# Add version info to all SUMMARY output

## Context
Summary headers currently show `{name} BUILD SUMMARY` etc. User wants package
versions displayed: `tspec v0.15.5 BUILD SUMMARY` in the header (from root
`[package]` version when present), and each row showing its package version
like `rlibc-x1 v0.2.0`. Virtual workspaces (no root `[package]`) omit the
header version.

## Changes

### 1. `src/workspace.rs` — Add version fields
- Add `pub version: String` to `PackageMember` — populated from `pkg.version` in `discover()`
- Add `pub version: Option<String>` to `WorkspaceInfo` — the root package's version (if root dir has a `[package]`); found by checking if any member's manifest path matches `workspace_root/Cargo.toml`
- Add `pub fn name_versioned(&self) -> String` method that returns `"{name} v{ver}"` or just `"{name}"` if version is None

### 2. `src/all.rs` — Thread version through summaries
- Add `pub version: String` to `OpResult` — set from `member.version` in `build_all()`, `test_all()`, `run_all()`, `compare_all()`
- Add `version: String` to `SummaryRow`
- Update `print_summary_table()`: append version to name in the Name column, e.g. `"rlibc-x1 v0.2.0"` (no separate column — keeps it simple)
- Update header: change `ws_name` parameter usage to accept the versioned name from callers
- Update `print_summary()`, `print_test_summary()`, `print_run_summary()`, `print_compare_summary()` — pass versioned workspace name

### 3. `src/compare.rs` — Single-package compare summary
- `print_comparison()` takes `pkg_name: &str` — callers need to pass versioned name
- Update `cmd/compare.rs` to look up version and format `"{pkg_name} v{ver}"`

### 4. `src/cmd/build.rs`, `src/cmd/test.rs`, `src/cmd/run.rs`, `src/cmd/compare.rs`
- All workspace-mode paths: use `workspace.name_versioned()` instead of `workspace.name()`

### 5. Update integration test
- `tests/integration_test.rs:553` — assertion checks `"TEST SUMMARY"` substring, should still pass

### 6. No README changes (per project conventions — only if explicitly requested)

## Example output

**POPWS (tspec — root has [package]):**
```
============================================
       tspec v0.15.6 BUILD SUMMARY
============================================
  Package                Status    Size
  tspec v0.15.6          [ OK ]    5.2M
  tspec-build v0.1.0     [ OK ]      --

  Build: 2 ok, 0 failed
============================================
```

**Virtual workspace (rlibc-x — no root [package]):**
```
============================================
          rlibc-x BUILD SUMMARY
============================================
  Package                  Status    Size
  rlibc-x1 v0.2.0         [ OK ]      --
  rlibc-x2 v0.3.0         [ OK ]      --

  Build: 2 ok, 0 failed
============================================
```

## Verification
```bash
tspec test -p tspec
tspec test -p tspec-build
tspec install --path .
tspec test -p tspec        # with new binary
tspec clippy
tspec fmt --check
```
Also manually verify: `tspec build` and `tspec test` show versions in output.

     1→//! Workspace discovery
     2→//!
     3→//! Uses `cargo metadata` to discover workspace members.
     4→
     5→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     6→use cargo_metadata::MetadataCommand;
     7→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     8→
     9→/// Information about a workspace package
    10→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    11→pub struct PackageMember {
    12→    pub name: String,
    13→    pub path: PathBuf,
    14→    pub has_binary: bool,
    15→}
    16→
    17→/// Workspace information from cargo metadata
    18→pub struct WorkspaceInfo {
    19→    pub root: PathBuf,
    20→    pub members: Vec<PackageMember>,
    21→}
    22→
    23→impl WorkspaceInfo {
    24→    /// Workspace name derived from the root directory basename
    25→    pub fn name(&self) -> &str {
    26→        self.root
    27→            .file_name()
    28→            .and_then(|n| n.to_str())
    29→            .unwrap_or("workspace")
    30→    }
    31→
    32→    /// Discover workspace using cargo metadata
    33→    pub fn discover(project_root: &Path) -> Result<Self> {
    34→        let metadata = MetadataCommand::new()
    35→            .manifest_path(project_root.join("Cargo.toml"))
    36→            .no_deps()
    37→            .exec()
    38→            .context("failed to run cargo metadata")?;
    39→
    40→        let root = metadata.workspace_root.as_std_path().to_path_buf();
    41→
    42→        let members: Vec<PackageMember> = metadata
    43→            .workspace_packages()
    44→            .iter()
    45→            .map(|pkg| {
    46→                let path = pkg
    47→                    .manifest_path
    48→                    .parent()
    49→                    .unwrap()
    50→                    .as_std_path()
    51→                    .to_path_buf();
    52→                let has_binary = pkg.targets.iter().any(|t| t.is_bin());
    53→
    54→                PackageMember {
    55→                    name: pkg.name.clone(),
    56→                    path,
    57→                    has_binary,
    58→                }
    59→            })
    60→            .collect();
    61→
    62→        Ok(WorkspaceInfo { root, members })
    63→    }
    64→
    65→    /// Get all workspace members
    66→    pub fn buildable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
    67→        self.members.iter().collect()
    68→    }
    69→
    70→    /// Get members that have binaries
    71→    pub fn runnable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
    72→        self.members.iter().filter(|m| m.has_binary).collect()
    73→    }
    74→}
    75→
    76→#[cfg(test)]
    77→mod tests {
    78→    use super::*;
    79→
    80→    #[test]
    81→    fn discover_works() {
    82→        // This test works for both workspaces and POPs
    83→        let root = crate::find_paths::find_project_root().unwrap();
    84→        let info = WorkspaceInfo::discover(&root);
    85→        if let Ok(info) = info {
    86→            assert!(!info.members.is_empty());
    87→
    88→            // All members should be buildable
    89→            assert_eq!(info.buildable_members().len(), info.members.len());
    90→
    91→            let _runnable = info.runnable_members();
    92→            // Just verify it doesn't panic - count depends on project structure
    93→        }
    94→        // If cargo metadata fails (e.g., not in a Rust project), that's OK for CI
    95→    }
    96→}
    97→

     1→//! Batch operations on all workspace packages
     2→//!
     3→//! Provides build_all, run_all, test_all for operating on all workspace members.
     4→
     5→use std::process::ExitCode;
     6→
     7→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     8→
     9→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
    10→use crate::cargo_build::{build_package, test_package};
    11→use crate::cmd::{TestResult, parse_test_results};
    12→use crate::compare::{SpecResult, compare_specs, print_comparison};
    13→use crate::find_paths::find_tspecs;
    14→use crate::run::run_binary;
    15→use crate::tspec::spec_name_from_path;
    16→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    17→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    18→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
    19→
    20→/// Normalize tspec patterns for per-package matching in all-packages mode.
    21→///
    22→/// Strips directory components so shell-expanded paths from the workspace root
    23→/// don't leak into sub-packages. Filters out non-tspec shell expansions
    24→/// (e.g. `target/`, `tools/`) — only `.ts.toml` files and glob patterns are kept.
    25→///
    26→/// Returns `None` if all patterns were filtered out (likely shell expansion of a
    27→/// glob that matched non-tspec entries). The caller should warn about quoting.
    28→fn normalize_tspec_patterns(patterns: &[String]) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    29→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    30→        return Some(Vec::new());
    31→    }
    32→    let filenames: Vec<String> = patterns
    33→        .iter()
    34→        .filter_map(|p| {
    35→            let name = Path::new(p)
    36→                .file_name()
    37→                .map(|f| f.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
    38→                .unwrap_or_else(|| p.clone());
    39→            // Keep glob patterns (unexpanded) and actual tspec files
    40→            let is_glob = name.contains('*') || name.contains('?') || name.contains('[');
    41→            let is_tspec = name.ends_with(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    42→            if is_glob || is_tspec {
    43→                Some(name)
    44→            } else {
    45→                None
    46→            }
    47→        })
    48→        .collect();
    49→    if filenames.is_empty() && !patterns.is_empty() {
    50→        None // all patterns were filtered — likely shell expansion
    51→    } else {
    52→        Some(filenames)
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→/// Warn that shell glob expansion likely ate the tspec pattern.
    57→pub fn warn_shell_glob_expansion(patterns: &[String]) {
    58→    eprintln!(
    59→        "Warning: -t arguments ({}) don't look like tspec files.",
    60→        patterns.join(", ")
    61→    );
    62→    eprintln!("  The shell likely expanded your glob before tspec could see it.");
    63→    eprintln!("  Quote the pattern to prevent shell expansion: -t 'pattern*'");
    64→}
    65→
    66→/// Resolve tspec patterns for a workspace member.
    67→///
    68→/// Returns the matching spec paths, or an empty vec if no patterns match.
    69→/// When `patterns` is empty, returns an empty vec (caller should use default behavior).
    70→fn resolve_specs_for_member(member_path: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    71→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    72→        return Vec::new();
    73→    }
    74→    find_tspecs(member_path, patterns).unwrap_or_default()
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Extract a short spec label from an optional tspec path.
    78→fn spec_label(tspec: &Option<String>) -> String {
    79→    match tspec {
    80→        Some(path) => spec_name_from_path(Path::new(path)),
    81→        None => String::new(),
    82→    }
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Result of a batch operation on a single package
    86→pub struct OpResult {
    87→    pub name: String,
    88→    pub spec: String,
    89→    pub success: bool,
    90→    pub message: String,
    91→    pub size: Option<u64>,
    92→    pub test_counts: Option<TestResult>,
    93→}
    94→
    95→/// Build all workspace packages (excluding build tools)
    96→///
    97→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
    98→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
    99→pub fn build_all(
   100→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   101→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   102→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   103→    strip: bool,
   104→    fail_fast: bool,
   105→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   106→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   107→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   108→        Some(n) => n,
   109→        None => {
   110→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   111→            return Vec::new();
   112→        }
   113→    };
   114→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   115→
   116→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   117→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   118→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   119→            continue;
   120→        }
   121→
   122→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   123→
   124→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   125→            vec![None]
   126→        } else {
   127→            specs
   128→                .into_iter()
   129→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   130→                .collect()
   131→        };
   132→
   133→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   134→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   135→            let result = match build_package(
   136→                &member.name,
   137→                tspec.as_deref(),
   138→                cli_profile,
   139→                &workspace.root,
   140→                flags,
   141→            ) {
   142→                Ok(build_result) => {
   143→                    if strip
   144→                        && member.has_binary
   145→                        && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)
   146→                    {
   147→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   148→                    }
   149→                    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path).ok();
   150→                    OpResult {
   151→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   152→                        spec,
   153→                        success: true,
   154→                        message: format!("{}", build_result.binary_path.display()),
   155→                        size,
   156→                        test_counts: None,
   157→                    }
   158→                }
   159→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   160→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   161→                    spec,
   162→                    success: false,
   163→                    message: e.to_string(),
   164→                    size: None,
   165→                    test_counts: None,
   166→                },
   167→            };
   168→
   169→            let failed = !result.success;
   170→            results.push(result);
   171→
   172→            if failed && fail_fast {
   173→                return results;
   174→            }
   175→        }
   176→    }
   177→
   178→    results
   179→}
   180→
   181→/// Run all app packages sequentially
   182→///
   183→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   184→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   185→pub fn run_all(
   186→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   187→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   188→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   189→    strip: bool,
   190→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   191→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   192→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   193→        Some(n) => n,
   194→        None => {
   195→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   196→            return Vec::new();
   197→        }
   198→    };
   199→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   200→
   201→    for member in workspace.runnable_members() {
   202→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   203→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   204→            continue;
   205→        }
   206→
   207→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   208→
   209→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   210→            vec![None]
   211→        } else {
   212→            specs
   213→                .into_iter()
   214→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   215→                .collect()
   216→        };
   217→
   218→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   219→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   220→            let result = match build_package(
   221→                &member.name,
   222→                tspec.as_deref(),
   223→                cli_profile,
   224→                &workspace.root,
   225→                flags,
   226→            ) {
   227→                Ok(build_result) => {
   228→                    if strip && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path) {
   229→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   230→                    }
   231→                    match run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[]) {
   232→                        Ok(exit_code) => OpResult {
   233→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   234→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   235→                            success: true,
   236→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   237→                            size: None,
   238→                            test_counts: None,
   239→                        },
   240→                        Err(e) => OpResult {
   241→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   242→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   243→                            success: false,
   244→                            message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   245→                            size: None,
   246→                            test_counts: None,
   247→                        },
   248→                    }
   249→                }
   250→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   251→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   252→                    spec,
   253→                    success: false,
   254→                    message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   255→                    size: None,
   256→                    test_counts: None,
   257→                },
   258→            };
   259→
   260→            results.push(result);
   261→        }
   262→    }
   263→
   264→    results
   265→}

   345→
   346→/// A row for the summary table: package name, spec, and pre-formatted detail string.
   347→struct SummaryRow {
   348→    name: String,
   349→    spec: String,
   350→    detail: String,
   351→}
   352→
   353→/// Print a summary table with consistent formatting across all operations.
   354→///
   355→/// - `ws_name` — workspace/package name for the header
   356→/// - `cmd` — operation name ("TEST", "BUILD", "RUN", "COMPARE")
   357→/// - `detail_header` — column header for the detail column (e.g. "Status", "Status    Size", "Exit")
   358→/// - `rows` — pre-formatted rows
   359→/// - `footer` — footer line (e.g. "Test: 3 passed, 1 failed")
   360→fn print_summary_table(
   361→    ws_name: &str,
   362→    cmd: &str,
   363→    detail_header: &str,
   364→    rows: &[SummaryRow],
   365→    footer: &str,
   366→) {
   367→    let max_name_len = rows.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(7).max(7);
   368→    let has_spec = rows.iter().any(|r| !r.spec.is_empty());
   369→    let max_spec_len = if has_spec {
   370→        rows.iter().map(|r| r.spec.len()).max().unwrap_or(4).max(4)
   371→    } else {
   372→        0
   373→    };
   374→
   375→    println!();
   376→    print_header!(format!("{ws_name} {cmd} SUMMARY"));
   377→    if has_spec {
   378→        println!(
   379→            "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {detail_header}",
   380→            "Package",
   381→            "Spec",
   382→            nw = max_name_len,
   383→            sw = max_spec_len
   384→        );
   385→    } else {
   386→        println!(
   387→            "  {:width$}  {detail_header}",
   388→            "Package",
   389→            width = max_name_len
   390→        );
   391→    }
   392→
   393→    for row in rows {
   394→        if has_spec {
   395→            println!(
   396→                "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {}",
   397→                row.name,
   398→                row.spec,
   399→                row.detail,
   400→                nw = max_name_len,
   401→                sw = max_spec_len
   402→            );
   403→        } else {
   404→            println!(
   405→                "  {:width$}  {}",
   406→                row.name,
   407→                row.detail,
   408→                width = max_name_len
   409→            );
   410→        }
   411→    }
   412→
   413→    println!();
   414→    if !footer.is_empty() {
   415→        println!("  {footer}");
   416→    }
   417→    print_hline!();
   418→    println!();
   419→}
   420→
   421→/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)
   422→pub fn print_test_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   423→    let mut pkg_passed = 0;
   424→    let mut pkg_failed = 0;
   425→    let mut total = TestResult::default();
   426→
   427→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   428→        .iter()
   429→        .map(|r| {
   430→            let detail = if r.success {
   431→                pkg_passed += 1;
   432→                if let Some(counts) = &r.test_counts {
   433→                    total.merge(counts);
   434→                    if counts.ignored > 0 {
   435→                        format!(
   436→                            "[PASS]  {} passed ({} ignored)",
   437→                            counts.passed, counts.ignored
   438→                        )
   439→                    } else {
   440→                        format!("[PASS]  {} passed", counts.passed)
   441→                    }
   442→                } else {
   443→                    "[PASS]".to_string()
   444→                }
   445→            } else {
   446→                pkg_failed += 1;
   447→                if let Some(counts) = &r.test_counts {
   448→                    total.merge(counts);
   449→                    if counts.ignored > 0 {
   450→                        format!(
   451→                            "[FAIL]  {} failed ({} ignored)",
   452→                            counts.failed, counts.ignored
   453→                        )
   454→                    } else {
   455→                        format!("[FAIL]  {} failed", counts.failed)
   456→                    }
   457→                } else {
   458→                    "[FAIL]".to_string()
   459→                }
   460→            };
   461→            SummaryRow {
   462→                name: r.name.clone(),
   463→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   464→                detail,
   465→            }
   466→        })
   467→        .collect();
   468→
   469→    let pkg_count = pkg_passed + pkg_failed;
   470→    let footer = if total.ignored > 0 {
   471→        format!(
   472→            "Test: {} packages, {} passed, {} failed ({} ignored)",
   473→            pkg_count, total.passed, total.failed, total.ignored
   474→        )
   475→    } else {
   476→        format!(
   477→            "Test: {} packages, {} passed, {} failed",
   478→            pkg_count, total.passed, total.failed
   479→        )
   480→    };
   481→
   482→    print_summary_table(name, "TEST", "Status", &rows, &footer);
   483→
   484→    if pkg_failed > 0 {
   485→        ExitCode::from(1)
   486→    } else {
   487→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   488→    }
   489→}
   490→
   491→/// Print a summary for build operations (OK/FAILED)
   492→pub fn print_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   493→    let mut ok_count = 0;
   494→    let mut failed_count = 0;
   495→
   496→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   497→        .iter()
   498→        .map(|r| {
   499→            let status = if r.success {
   500→                ok_count += 1;
   501→                "[ OK ]"
   502→            } else {
   503→                failed_count += 1;
   504→                "[FAIL]"
   505→            };
   506→            let size_str = r.size.map(format_size).unwrap_or_else(|| "--".to_string());
   507→            SummaryRow {
   508→                name: r.name.clone(),
   509→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   510→                detail: format!("{status}  {size_str:>6}"),
   511→            }
   512→        })
   513→        .collect();
   514→
   515→    print_summary_table(
   516→        name,
   517→        "BUILD",
   518→        "Status    Size",
   519→        &rows,
   520→        &format!("Build: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   521→    );
   522→
   523→    if failed_count > 0 {
   524→        ExitCode::from(1)
   525→    } else {
   526→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   527→    }
   528→}
   529→
   530→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   531→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   532→        format!("{:.1}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   533→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   534→        format!("{:.1}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   535→    } else {
   536→        format!("{}", bytes)
   537→    }
   538→}
   539→
   540→/// Result of a compare operation on a single package
   541→pub struct CompareResult {
   542→    pub op: OpResult,
   543→    pub specs: Vec<SpecResult>,
   544→}
   545→
   546→/// Compare all workspace packages that have binaries
   547→///
   548→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package discovers its own specs via default glob.
   549→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   550→pub fn compare_all(
   551→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   552→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   553→    fail_fast: bool,
   554→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   555→) -> Vec<CompareResult> {
   556→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   557→        Some(n) => n,
   558→        None => {
   559→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   560→            return Vec::new();
   561→        }
   562→    };
   563→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   564→
   565→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   566→        if !member.has_binary {
   567→            continue;
   568→        }
   569→
   570→        let spec_paths = if normalized.is_empty() {
   571→            find_tspecs(&member.path, &[]).unwrap_or_default()
   572→        } else {
   573→            let resolved = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   574→            if resolved.is_empty() {
   575→                continue;
   576→            }
   577→            resolved
   578→        };
   579→
   580→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   581→
   582→        let (op, specs) = match compare_specs(&member.name, &spec_paths, &workspace.root, flags) {
   583→            Ok(spec_results) => (
   584→                OpResult {
   585→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   586→                    spec: String::new(),
   587→                    success: true,
   588→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   589→                    size: None,
   590→                    test_counts: None,
   591→                },
   592→                spec_results,
   593→            ),
   594→            Err(e) => (
   595→                OpResult {
   596→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   597→                    spec: String::new(),
   598→                    success: false,
   599→                    message: e.to_string(),
   600→                    size: None,
   601→                    test_counts: None,
   602→                },
   603→                Vec::new(),
   604→            ),
   605→        };
   606→
   607→        let failed = !op.success;
   608→        results.push(CompareResult { op, specs });
   609→
   610→        if failed && fail_fast {
   611→            break;
   612→        }
   613→    }
   614→
   615→    results
   616→}
   617→
   618→/// Print a summary for compare operations
   619→///
   620→/// With a single package, just prints its comparison table.
   621→/// With multiple packages, reprints all per-package tables then an overall OK/FAIL summary.
   622→pub fn print_compare_summary(name: &str, results: &[CompareResult]) -> ExitCode {
   623→    let has_failure = results.iter().any(|r| !r.op.success);
   624→
   625→    // Reprint per-package comparison tables together
   626→    for result in results {
   627→        if !result.specs.is_empty() {
   628→            print_comparison(&result.op.name, &result.specs);
   629→        }
   630→    }
   631→
   632→    // Only show overall COMPARE SUMMARY when there are multiple packages
   633→    if results.len() > 1 {
   634→        let mut ok_count = 0;
   635→        let mut failed_count = 0;
   636→
   637→        let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   638→            .iter()
   639→            .map(|r| {
   640→                let detail = if r.op.success {
   641→                    ok_count += 1;
   642→                    "[ OK ]".to_string()
   643→                } else {
   644→                    failed_count += 1;
   645→                    "[FAIL]".to_string()
   646→                };
   647→                SummaryRow {
   648→                    name: r.op.name.clone(),
   649→                    spec: r.op.spec.clone(),
   650→                    detail,
   651→                }
   652→            })
   653→            .collect();
   654→
   655→        print_summary_table(
   656→            name,
   657→            "COMPARE",
   658→            "Status",
   659→            &rows,
   660→            &format!("Compare: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   661→        );
   662→    }
   663→
   664→    if has_failure {
   665→        ExitCode::from(1)
   666→    } else {
   667→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   668→    }
   669→}
   670→
   671→/// Print a summary for run operations (shows exit codes, not pass/fail)
   672→pub fn print_run_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   673→    let mut error_count = 0;
   674→
   675→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   676→        .iter()
   677→        .map(|r| {
   678→            let detail = if r.success {
   679→                let code = r.message.strip_prefix("exit code: ").unwrap_or(&r.message);
   680→                format!("{code:>4}")
   681→            } else {
   682→                error_count += 1;
   683→                format!("ERROR: {}", r.message)
   684→            };
   685→            SummaryRow {
   686→                name: r.name.clone(),
   687→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   688→                detail,
   689→            }
   690→        })
   691→        .collect();
   692→
   693→    let footer = if error_count > 0 {
   694→        format!("Run: {error_count} error(s)")
   695→    } else {
   696→        String::new()
   697→    };
   698→
   699→    print_summary_table(name, "RUN", "Exit", &rows, &footer);
   700→
   701→    if error_count > 0 {
   702→        ExitCode::from(1)
   703→    } else {
   704→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   705→    }
   706→}
   707→

    14→/// Build package(s) with a translation spec
    15→#[derive(Args)]
    16→pub struct BuildCmd {
    17→    /// Package to build (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    18→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    19→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    20→    /// Package to build (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    21→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    22→    pub package: Option<String>,
    23→    /// Build all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    24→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    25→    pub workspace: bool,
    26→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to build with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    27→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    28→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    29→    /// Release build
    30→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    31→    pub release: bool,
    32→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    33→    #[arg(long)]
    34→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    35→    /// Strip symbols from binary after build
    36→    #[arg(short, long)]
    37→    pub strip: bool,
    38→    /// Stop on first failure (for all-packages mode)
    39→    #[arg(short, long)]
    40→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    41→}
    42→
    43→impl BuildCmd {
    44→    /// Resolve the effective profile from --release / --profile flags.
    45→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    46→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    47→            Some(p)
    48→        } else if self.release {
    49→            Some("release")
    50→        } else {
    51→            None
    52→        }
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→impl Execute for BuildCmd {
    57→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    58→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    59→
    60→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    61→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    62→            None
    63→        } else {
    64→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    65→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg, project_root)?,
    66→                None => current_package_name(project_root),
    67→            }
    68→        };
    69→
    70→        match resolved {
    71→            None => {
    72→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover(project_root)?;
    73→                let results = build_all(
    74→                    &workspace,
    75→                    &self.tspec,
    76→                    cli_profile,
    77→                    self.strip,
    78→                    self.fail_fast,
    79→                    flags,
    80→                );
    81→                Ok(print_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    82→            }
    83→            Some(name) => {
    84→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    85→                    let result = build_package(&name, None, cli_profile, project_root, flags)?;
    86→                    if self.strip {
    87→                        strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    88→                    }
    89→                } else {
    90→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
    91→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    92→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
    93→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
    94→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    95→                        let result = build_package(
    96→                            &pkg_name,
    97→                            Some(&spec_str),
    98→                            cli_profile,
    99→                            project_root,
   100→                            flags,
   101→                        )?;
   102→                        if self.strip {
   103→                            strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
   104→                        }
   105→                    }
   106→                }
   107→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   108→            }
   109→        }
   110→    }
   111→}

    15→pub struct TestResult {

    19→    pub filtered: u32,
    20→}
    21→
    22→impl TestResult {
    23→    /// Aggregate another TestResult into this one.
    24→    pub fn merge(&mut self, other: &TestResult) {
    25→        self.passed += other.passed;
    26→        self.failed += other.failed;
    27→        self.ignored += other.ignored;
    28→        self.filtered += other.filtered;
    29→    }
    30→
    31→    /// Total tests that actually ran (passed + failed).
    32→    pub fn total_ran(&self) -> u32 {
    33→        self.passed + self.failed
    34→    }
    35→}
    36→
    37→/// Parse a `test result:` line from cargo test output.
    38→///
    39→/// Example: `test result: ok. 263 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.42s`
    40→pub fn parse_test_result_line(line: &str) -> Option<TestResult> {
    41→    let rest = line.strip_prefix("test result:")?;
    42→    let rest = rest.trim();
    43→    // Skip the "ok." or "FAILED." prefix
    44→    let rest = rest
    45→        .strip_prefix("ok.")
    46→        .or_else(|| rest.strip_prefix("FAILED."))?;
    47→    let rest = rest.trim();
    48→
    49→    let mut result = TestResult::default();
    50→    for part in rest.split(';') {
    51→        let part = part.trim();
    52→        if let Some((num, label)) = part.split_once(' ') {
    53→            let Ok(n) = num.parse::<u32>() else {
    54→                continue;
    55→            };
    56→            match label.trim() {
    57→                "passed" => result.passed = n,
    58→                "failed" => result.failed = n,
    59→                "ignored" => result.ignored = n,
    60→                "filtered" | "filtered out" => result.filtered = n,
    61→                _ => {} // "measured"
    62→            }
    63→        }
    64→    }
    65→    Some(result)
    66→}
    67→
    68→/// Parse and aggregate raw `test result:` lines into a single TestResult.
    69→pub fn parse_test_results(lines: &[String]) -> TestResult {
    70→    let mut aggregated = TestResult::default();
    71→    for line in lines {
    72→        if let Some(parsed) = parse_test_result_line(line) {
    73→            aggregated.merge(&parsed);
    74→        }
    75→    }
    76→    aggregated
    77→}
    78→
    79→/// Test package(s) with a translation spec
    80→#[derive(Args)]
    81→pub struct TestCmd {
    82→    /// Package to test (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    83→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    84→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    85→    /// Package to test (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    86→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    87→    pub package: Option<String>,
    88→    /// Test all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    89→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    90→    pub workspace: bool,
    91→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to test with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    92→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    93→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    94→    /// Release build
    95→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    96→    pub release: bool,
    97→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    98→    #[arg(long)]
    99→    pub profile: Option<String>,
   100→    /// Stop on first failure
   101→    #[arg(short, long)]
   102→    pub fail_fast: bool,
   103→    /// List test targets and test functions
   104→    #[arg(short, long)]
   105→    pub list: bool,
   106→    /// List available target names for use with --test
   107→    #[arg(long = "target-names")]
   108→    pub target_names: bool,
   109→    /// Filter by qualified test name, e.g. '-n _by_' matches 'ts_cmd::remove::tests::remove_by_index'
   110→    #[arg(short = 'n', long = "names", num_args = 1..)]
   111→    pub name_filter: Vec<String>,
   112→    /// Run only the named test target (use --target-names to see available names; repeatable)
   113→    #[arg(long = "test", value_name = "TARGET-NAME")]
   114→    pub test_target: Vec<String>,
   115→    /// Include #[ignore]'d tests (equivalent to -- --include-ignored)
   116→    #[arg(long = "all-tests")]
   117→    pub all_tests: bool,
   118→    /// Extra arguments passed after -- to the test binary (e.g., --ignored)
   119→    #[arg(last = true)]
   120→    pub test_args: Vec<String>,
   121→}
   122→
   123→impl TestCmd {
   124→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
   125→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
   126→            Some(p)
   127→        } else if self.release {
   128→            Some("release")
   129→        } else {
   130→            None
   131→        }
   132→    }
   133→}
   134→
   135→impl Execute for TestCmd {
   136→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   137→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
   138→
   139→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
   140→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
   141→            None
   142→        } else {
   143→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
   144→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg, project_root)?,
   145→                None => current_package_name(project_root),
   146→            }
   147→        };
   148→
   149→        if self.target_names {
   150→            return list_target_names(resolved.as_deref(), project_root, flags);
   151→        }
   152→
   153→        if self.list {
   154→            return list_tests(resolved.as_deref(), &self.name_filter, project_root, flags);
   155→        }
   156→
   157→        // Build extra_args from --names, --test, --all-tests, and trailing args
   158→        let has_extra = !self.name_filter.is_empty()
   159→            || !self.test_target.is_empty()
   160→            || !self.test_args.is_empty()
   161→            || self.all_tests;
   162→        let flags = if has_extra {
   163→            let mut f = flags.clone();
   164→            for target in &self.test_target {
   165→                f.extra_args.push("--test".to_string());
   166→                f.extra_args.push(target.clone());
   167→            }
   168→            // Name filters, --all-tests, and test_args all go after --
   169→            // The test harness accepts [FILTERS...] as OR-matched substrings
   170→            if !self.name_filter.is_empty() || !self.test_args.is_empty() || self.all_tests {
   171→                f.extra_args.push("--".to_string());
   172→                f.extra_args.extend(self.name_filter.clone());
   173→                if self.all_tests {
   174→                    f.extra_args.push("--include-ignored".to_string());
   175→                }
   176→                f.extra_args.extend(self.test_args.clone());
   177→            }
   178→            f
   179→        } else {
   180→            flags.clone()
   181→        };
   182→
   183→        match resolved {
   184→            None => {
   185→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover(project_root)?;
   186→
   187→                // When --test targets are specified in workspace mode, only test
   188→                // packages that actually have matching files in tests/
   189→                if !self.test_target.is_empty() {
   190→                    let members = workspace.buildable_members();
   191→                    let matching: Vec<_> = members
   192→                        .iter()
   193→                        .filter(|m| {
   194→                            self.test_target
   195→                                .iter()
   196→                                .any(|t| m.path.join("tests").join(format!("{t}.rs")).exists())
   197→                        })
   198→                        .collect();
   199→
   200→                    if matching.is_empty() {
   201→                        eprintln!(
   202→                            "No packages contain test targets: {}",
   203→                            self.test_target.join(", ")
   204→                        );
   205→                        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   206→                    }
   207→
   208→                    for member in &matching {
   209→                        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   210→                        test_package(&member.name, None, cli_profile, project_root, &flags)?;
   211→                    }
   212→                    return Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS);
   213→                }
   214→
   215→                let results =
   216→                    test_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.fail_fast, &flags);
   217→                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
   218→            }
   219→            Some(name) => {
   220→                let mut all_lines = Vec::new();
   221→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
   222→                    all_lines = test_package(&name, None, cli_profile, project_root, &flags)?;
   223→                } else {
   224→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
   225→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
   226→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
   227→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
   228→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
   229→                        let lines = test_package(
   230→                            &pkg_name,
   231→                            Some(&spec_str),
   232→                            cli_profile,
   233→                            project_root,
   234→                            &flags,
   235→                        )?;
   236→                        all_lines.extend(lines);
   237→                    }
   238→                }
   239→                let counts = parse_test_results(&all_lines);
   240→                if counts.total_ran() == 0 {
   241→                    eprintln!("Error: 0 tests ran (filter may not match anything)");
   242→                    return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   243→                }
   244→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   245→            }
   246→        }
   247→    }
   248→}
   249→
   250→/// Format a cargo test target header into a human-friendly label.
   251→///
   252→/// Cargo emits headers like:
   253→/// - `unittests src/lib.rs (target/debug/deps/tspec-abc123)` → unit tests (no --test name)
   254→/// - `tests/tspec_test.rs (target/debug/deps/tspec_test-abc123)` → integration test
   255→///
   256→/// For integration tests, we show the `--test` usable name prominently.
   257→fn format_target_header(raw: &str) -> String {
   258→    // Strip the "(target/...)" suffix for cleaner output
   259→    let label = if let Some(idx) = raw.find(" (") {
   260→        &raw[..idx]
   261→    } else {
   262→        raw
   263→    };
   264→
   265→    if label.starts_with("unittests ") {
   266→        // "unittests src/lib.rs" → unit tests, not selectable with --test
   267→        format!("unit tests ({})", label.strip_prefix("unittests ").unwrap())
   268→    } else if label.starts_with("tests/") {
   269→        // "tests/tspec_test.rs" → extract basename for --test
   270→        let basename = label
   271→            .strip_prefix("tests/")
   272→            .and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix(".rs"))
   273→            .unwrap_or(label);
   274→        format!("{} (--test {})", label, basename)
   275→    } else {
   276→        label.to_string()
   277→    }
   278→}
   279→
   280→/// List available target names for `--test`.
   281→///
   282→/// Runs `cargo test --no-run` and parses stderr for "Running tests/..." lines,
   283→/// extracting the basename (without .rs) as the target name.
   284→fn list_target_names(
   285→    package: Option<&str>,
   286→    project_root: &Path,
   287→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   288→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   289→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
   290→    cmd.arg("test").arg("--no-run");
   291→    if let Some(pkg) = package {
   292→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(pkg);
   293→    }
   294→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   295→    cmd.current_dir(project_root);
   296→
   297→    let output = cmd.output().context("failed to run cargo test --no-run")?;
   298→
   299→    if !output.status.success() {
   300→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   301→        eprintln!("{}", stderr);
   302→        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   303→    }
   304→
   305→    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   306→    let mut found = false;
   307→
   308→    println!("Available target names for --test:");
   309→    for line in stderr.lines() {
   310→        let trimmed = line.trim();
   311→        // --no-run emits "Executable tests/foo.rs (target/...)" for integration tests
   312→        if let Some(rest) = trimmed
   313→            .strip_prefix("Executable tests/")
   314→            .or_else(|| trimmed.strip_prefix("Running tests/"))
   315→        {
   316→            let file = if let Some(idx) = rest.find(" (") {
   317→                &rest[..idx]
   318→            } else {
   319→                rest
   320→            };
   321→            if let Some(name) = file.strip_suffix(".rs") {
   322→                println!("  {}    (tests/{})", name, file);
   323→                found = true;
   324→            }
   325→        }
   326→    }
   327→
   328→    if !found {
   329→        println!("  (none — no integration test files in tests/)");
   330→    }
   331→
   332→    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   333→}
   334→
   335→/// Run `cargo test -- --list` and format the output.
   336→///
   337→/// Groups test functions under their target headers, showing counts per target
   338→/// and a total. Skips targets with zero tests.
   339→fn list_tests(
   340→    package: Option<&str>,
   341→    name_filter: &[String],
   342→    project_root: &Path,
   343→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   344→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   345→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
   346→    cmd.arg("test");
   347→    if let Some(pkg) = package {
   348→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(pkg);
   349→    }
   350→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   351→    cmd.arg("--");
   352→    cmd.args(name_filter);
   353→    cmd.arg("--list");
   354→    cmd.current_dir(project_root);
   355→
   356→    let output = cmd.output().context("failed to run cargo test -- --list")?;
   357→
   358→    if !output.status.success() {
   359→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   360→        eprintln!("{}", stderr);
   361→        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   362→    }
   363→
   364→    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   365→    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
   366→
   367→    // Parse target headers from stderr ("Running ..." and "Doc-tests ..." lines)
   368→    // Produce human-friendly labels that show the --test name when applicable.
   369→    let targets: Vec<String> = stderr
   370→        .lines()
   371→        .filter_map(|line| {
   372→            let trimmed = line.trim();
   373→            if trimmed.starts_with("Running ") {
   374→                let rest = trimmed.strip_prefix("Running ").unwrap();
   375→                Some(format_target_header(rest))
   376→            } else if trimmed.starts_with("Doc-tests ") {
   377→                Some(trimmed.to_string())
   378→            } else {
   379→                None
   380→            }
   381→        })
   382→        .collect();
   383→
   384→    // Parse stdout into groups split by "N tests, M benchmarks" summary lines
   385→    let mut groups: Vec<Vec<&str>> = Vec::new();
   386→    let mut current: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
   387→    for line in stdout.lines() {
   388→        if line.contains("tests,") && line.contains("benchmarks") {
   389→            groups.push(std::mem::take(&mut current));
   390→        } else if line.ends_with(": test") || line.ends_with(": bench") {
   391→            current.push(line);
   392→        }
   393→    }
   394→
   395→    // Print grouped output
   396→    let mut total = 0;
   397→    for (i, tests) in groups.iter().enumerate() {
   398→        if tests.is_empty() {
   399→            continue;
   400→        }
   401→        let unknown = "unknown target".to_string();
   402→        let header = targets.get(i).unwrap_or(&unknown);
   403→        println!("{} ({} tests)", header, tests.len());
   404→        for test in tests {
   405→            // Strip ": test" / ": bench" suffix for cleaner output
   406→            let name = test
   407→                .strip_suffix(": test")
   408→                .or_else(|| test.strip_suffix(": bench"))
   409→                .unwrap_or(test);
   410→            println!("  {}", name);
   411→        }
   412→        total += tests.len();
   413→        println!();
   414→    }
   415→
   416→    println!("{} tests total", total);
   417→    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   418→}
   419→
   420→#[cfg(test)]
   421→mod tests {
   422→    use super::*;
   423→    use crate::cli::Cli;
   424→    use clap::Parser;
   425→
   426→    fn parse(args: &[&str]) -> TestCmd {
   427→        let mut full = vec!["tspec", "test"];
   428→        full.extend_from_slice(args);
   429→        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(full).unwrap();
   430→        match cli.command {
   431→            crate::cli::Commands::Test(cmd) => cmd,
   432→            _ => panic!("expected Test command"),
   433→        }
   434→    }
   435→
   436→    #[test]
   437→    fn package_optional() {
   438→        let cmd = parse(&[]);
   439→        assert!(cmd.package.is_none());
   440→    }
   441→
   442→    #[test]
   443→    fn package_explicit() {
   444→        let cmd = parse(&["-p", "myapp"]);
   445→        assert_eq!(cmd.package.as_deref(), Some("myapp"));
   446→    }
   447→
   448→    #[test]
   449→    fn tspec_empty_by_default() {
   450→        let cmd = parse(&[]);
   451→        assert!(cmd.tspec.is_empty());
   452→    }
   453→
   454→    #[test]
   455→    fn tspec_single_file() {
   456→        let cmd = parse(&["-t", "foo.ts.toml"]);
   457→        assert_eq!(cmd.tspec, vec!["foo.ts.toml"]);
   458→    }
   459→
   460→    #[test]
   461→    fn tspec_multiple_values_one_flag() {
   462→        let cmd = parse(&["-t", "a.ts.toml", "b.ts.toml"]);
   463→        assert_eq!(cmd.tspec, vec!["a.ts.toml", "b.ts.toml"]);
   464→    }
   465→
   466→    #[test]
   467→    fn tspec_multiple_flags() {
   468→        let cmd = parse(&["-t", "a.ts.toml", "-t", "b.ts.toml"]);
   469→        assert_eq!(cmd.tspec, vec!["a.ts.toml", "b.ts.toml"]);
   470→    }
   471→
   472→    #[test]
   473→    fn all_flags_together() {
   474→        let cmd = parse(&["-p", "myapp", "-t", "spec.ts.toml"]);
   475→        assert_eq!(cmd.package.as_deref(), Some("myapp"));
   476→        assert_eq!(cmd.tspec, vec!["spec.ts.toml"]);
   477→    }
   478→
   479→    #[test]
   480→    fn workspace_flag_short() {
   481→        let cmd = parse(&["-w"]);
   482→        assert!(cmd.workspace);
   483→    }
   484→
   485→    #[test]
   486→    fn workspace_flag_long() {
   487→        let cmd = parse(&["--workspace"]);
   488→        assert!(cmd.workspace);
   489→    }
   490→
   491→    #[test]
   492→    fn workspace_default_false() {
   493→        let cmd = parse(&[]);
   494→        assert!(!cmd.workspace);
   495→    }
   496→
   497→    #[test]
   498→    fn fail_fast_flag() {
   499→        let cmd = parse(&["-w", "-f"]);
   500→        assert!(cmd.workspace);
   501→        assert!(cmd.fail_fast);
   502→    }
   503→
   504→    #[test]
   505→    fn fail_fast_long() {
   506→        let cmd = parse(&["--workspace", "--fail-fast"]);
   507→        assert!(cmd.workspace);
   508→        assert!(cmd.fail_fast);
   509→    }
   510→
   511→    #[test]
   512→    fn release_flag() {
   513→        let cmd = parse(&["-r"]);
   514→        assert!(cmd.release);
   515→    }
   516→
   517→    #[test]
   518→    fn profile_flag() {
   519→        let cmd = parse(&["--profile", "release-small"]);
   520→        assert_eq!(cmd.profile.as_deref(), Some("release-small"));
   521→        assert_eq!(cmd.effective_profile(), Some("release-small"));
   522→    }
   523→
   524→    #[test]
   525→    fn profile_and_release_conflict() {
   526→        let result = Cli::try_parse_from(["tspec", "test", "-r", "--profile", "custom"]);
   527→        assert!(result.is_err());
   528→    }
   529→
   530→    #[test]
   531→    fn target_names_flag() {
   532→        let cmd = parse(&["--target-names"]);
   533→        assert!(cmd.target_names);
   534→    }
   535→
   536→    #[test]
   537→    fn name_filter_single() {
   538→        let cmd = parse(&["-n", "my_test"]);
   539→        assert_eq!(cmd.name_filter, vec!["my_test"]);
   540→    }
   541→
   542→    #[test]
   543→    fn name_filter_long() {
   544→        let cmd = parse(&["--names", "my_test"]);
   545→        assert_eq!(cmd.name_filter, vec!["my_test"]);
   546→    }
   547→
   548→    #[test]
   549→    fn name_filter_multiple() {
   550→        let cmd = parse(&["-n", "foo", "bar"]);
   551→        assert_eq!(cmd.name_filter, vec!["foo", "bar"]);
   552→    }
   553→
   554→    #[test]
   555→    fn name_filter_with_package() {
   556→        let cmd = parse(&["-p", "myapp", "-n", "spec_default"]);
   557→        assert_eq!(cmd.package.as_deref(), Some("myapp"));
   558→        assert_eq!(cmd.name_filter, vec!["spec_default"]);
   559→    }
   560→
   561→    #[test]
   562→    fn test_target_flag() {
   563→        let cmd = parse(&["--test", "integration_test"]);
   564→        assert_eq!(cmd.test_target, vec!["integration_test"]);
   565→    }
   566→
   567→    #[test]
   568→    fn test_target_multiple() {
   569→        let cmd = parse(&["--test", "foo", "--test", "bar"]);
   570→        assert_eq!(cmd.test_target, vec!["foo", "bar"]);
   571→    }
   572→
   573→    #[test]
   574→    fn trailing_args() {
   575→        let cmd = parse(&["--", "--ignored"]);
   576→        assert_eq!(cmd.test_args, vec!["--ignored"]);
   577→    }
   578→
   579→    #[test]
   580→    fn test_target_and_trailing_args() {
   581→        let cmd = parse(&[
   582→            "--test",
   583→            "integration_test",
   584→            "--",
   585→            "--ignored",
   586→            "--nocapture",
   587→        ]);
   588→        assert_eq!(cmd.test_target, vec!["integration_test"]);
   589→        assert_eq!(cmd.test_args, vec!["--ignored", "--nocapture"]);
   590→    }
   591→
   592→    #[test]
   593→    fn test_target_with_package() {
   594→        let cmd = parse(&[
   595→            "-p",
   596→            "tspec",
   597→            "--test",
   598→            "integration_test",
   599→            "--",
   600→            "--ignored",
   601→        ]);
   602→        assert_eq!(cmd.package.as_deref(), Some("tspec"));
   603→        assert_eq!(cmd.test_target, vec!["integration_test"]);
   604→        assert_eq!(cmd.test_args, vec!["--ignored"]);

     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::Path;
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::{Execute, current_package_name, resolve_package_arg};
     7→use crate::all::{print_run_summary, run_all};
     8→use crate::binary::strip_binary;
     9→use crate::cargo_build::build_package;
    10→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
    11→use crate::run::run_binary;
    12→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    13→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    14→
    15→/// Build and run package(s) with a translation spec
    16→#[derive(Args)]
    17→pub struct RunCmd {
    18→    /// Package to run (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    19→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    20→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    21→    /// Package to run (defaults to current directory or all apps)
    22→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    23→    pub package: Option<String>,
    24→    /// Run all workspace apps (even when in a package directory)
    25→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    26→    pub workspace: bool,
    27→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to run with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    28→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    29→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    30→    /// Release build
    31→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    32→    pub release: bool,
    33→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    34→    #[arg(long)]
    35→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    36→    /// Strip symbols from binary before running
    37→    #[arg(short, long)]
    38→    pub strip: bool,
    39→    /// Arguments to pass to the binary (after --)
    40→    #[arg(last = true)]
    41→    pub args: Vec<String>,
    42→}
    43→
    44→impl RunCmd {
    45→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    46→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    47→            Some(p)
    48→        } else if self.release {
    49→            Some("release")
    50→        } else {
    51→            None
    52→        }
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→impl Execute for RunCmd {
    57→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    58→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    59→
    60→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    61→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    62→            None
    63→        } else {
    64→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    65→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg, project_root)?,
    66→                None => current_package_name(project_root),
    67→            }
    68→        };
    69→
    70→        match resolved {
    71→            None => {
    72→                // Run all apps (args not supported for --workspace)
    73→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover(project_root)?;
    74→                let results = run_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.strip, flags);
    75→                Ok(print_run_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    76→            }
    77→            Some(name) => {
    78→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    79→                    // Build, optionally strip, then run
    80→                    let result = build_package(&name, None, cli_profile, project_root, flags)?;
    81→                    if self.strip {
    82→                        strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    83→                    }
    84→                    let exit_code = run_binary(&result.binary_path, &self.args)?;
    85→                    std::process::exit(exit_code);
    86→                } else {
    87→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
    88→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    89→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
    90→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
    91→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    92→                        let result = build_package(
    93→                            &pkg_name,
    94→                            Some(&spec_str),
    95→                            cli_profile,
    96→                            project_root,
    97→                            flags,
    98→                        )?;
    99→                        if self.strip {
   100→                            strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
   101→                        }
   102→                        let exit_code = run_binary(&result.binary_path, &self.args)?;
   103→                        if exit_code != 0 {
   104→                            std::process::exit(exit_code);
   105→                        }
   106→                    }
   107→                    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   108→                }
   109→            }
   110→        }
   111→    }
   112→}

    15→pub struct CompareCmd {

    26→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]

    33→impl Execute for CompareCmd {
    34→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    35→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    36→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    37→            None
    38→        } else {
    39→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {

    41→                None => current_package_name(project_root),
    42→            }

    45→        match resolved {

    47→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover(project_root)?;
    48→                let results = compare_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, self.fail_fast, flags);
    49→                Ok(print_compare_summary(workspace.name(), &results))

    51→            Some(pkg_name) => {

    54→                let spec_paths = if self.tspec.is_empty() {

    56→                } else {

    59→                let results = compare_specs(&pkg_name, &spec_paths, project_root, flags)?;
    60→                print_comparison(&pkg_name, &results);
    61→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)

    63→        }
    64→    }

     1→use std::path::Path;
     2→
     3→use anyhow::Result;
     4→
     5→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
     6→use crate::cargo_build::{build_package, plain_cargo_build_release};
     7→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
     8→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
     9→
    10→/// Result of building a spec
    11→pub struct SpecResult {
    12→    pub name: String,
    13→    pub size: u64,
    14→}
    15→
    16→/// Compare multiple specs for a package
    17→pub fn compare_specs(
    18→    pkg_name: &str,
    19→    spec_paths: &[impl AsRef<Path> + std::fmt::Debug],
    20→    project_root: &Path,
    21→    flags: &CargoFlags,
    22→) -> Result<Vec<SpecResult>> {
    23→    println!("Comparing {} builds:\n", pkg_name);
    24→
    25→    let mut results = Vec::new();
    26→
    27→    // Always build cargo --release baseline first (unstripped + stripped)
    28→    match build_baseline(pkg_name, project_root, flags) {
    29→        Ok((size, stripped_size)) => {
    30→            results.push(SpecResult {
    31→                name: "cargo --release".to_string(),
    32→                size,
    33→            });
    34→            results.push(SpecResult {
    35→                name: "cargo --release-strip".to_string(),
    36→                size: stripped_size,
    37→            });
    38→        }
    39→        Err(_) => {
    40→            println!("    baseline build failed, skipping");
    41→        }
    42→    }
    43→    println!();
    44→
    45→    for spec_path in spec_paths {
    46→        let spec_path = spec_path.as_ref();
    47→        let name = spec_path
    48→            .file_name()
    49→            .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())
    50→            .unwrap_or_else(|| spec_path.display().to_string());
    51→
    52→        let size = build_spec(pkg_name, spec_path, project_root, flags)?;
    53→        results.push(SpecResult { name, size });
    54→        println!();
    55→    }
    56→
    57→    // Sort by size (smallest first)
    58→    results.sort_by_key(|r| r.size);
    59→
    60→    Ok(results)
    61→}
    62→
    63→/// Build baseline and return (unstripped_size, stripped_size)
    64→fn build_baseline(pkg_name: &str, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<(u64, u64)> {
    65→    println!("  cargo --release:");
    66→
    67→    let build_result = plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name, project_root, flags)?;
    68→
    69→    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    70→    println!("    size: {} bytes", format_size(size));
    71→
    72→    strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    73→    let stripped_size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    74→
    75→    Ok((size, stripped_size))
    76→}
    77→
    78→fn build_spec(
    79→    pkg_name: &str,
    80→    spec_path: &Path,
    81→    project_root: &Path,
    82→    flags: &CargoFlags,
    83→) -> Result<u64> {
    84→    let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    85→    println!(
    86→        "  {}:",
    87→        spec_path.file_name().unwrap_or_default().to_string_lossy()
    88→    );
    89→
    90→    // Build using spec settings (profile, strip, etc. are all in the spec)
    91→    let build_result = build_package(pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), None, project_root, flags)?;
    92→
    93→    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    94→    println!("    size: {} bytes", format_size(size));
    95→
    96→    Ok(size)
    97→}
    98→
    99→pub fn print_comparison(pkg_name: &str, results: &[SpecResult]) {
   100→    let largest_size = results.iter().map(|r| r.size).max().unwrap_or(0);
   101→    let max_name_len = results.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(4);
   102→
   103→    // Format percent change: show reduction with minus sign, baseline as 0.0%
   104→    let fmt_pct = |size: u64| -> String {
   105→        if largest_size == 0 {
   106→            return "   0.0%".to_string();
   107→        }
   108→        let pct = ((largest_size as f64 - size as f64) / largest_size as f64) * 100.0;
   109→        if pct > 0.0 {
   110→            format!("{:>7.1}%", -pct)
   111→        } else {
   112→            "   0.0%".to_string()
   113→        }
   114→    };
   115→
   116→    println!();
   117→    print_header!(format!("{} COMPARE SUMMARY", pkg_name));
   118→    println!(
   119→        "  {:width$}  {:>10}  {:>8}",
   120→        "Spec",
   121→        "Size",
   122→        "Change",
   123→        width = max_name_len
   124→    );
   125→    for result in results {
   126→        println!(
   127→            "  {:width$}  {:>10}  {}",
   128→            result.name,
   129→            format_size(result.size),
   130→            fmt_pct(result.size),
   131→            width = max_name_len
   132→        );
   133→    }
   134→    print_hline!();
   135→    println!();
   136→}
   137→
   138→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   139→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   140→        format!("{:.3}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   141→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   142→        format!("{:.3}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   143→    } else {
   144→        format!("{}", bytes)
   145→    }
   146→}
   147→
```

  "new_string": "        let root_manifest = root.join(\"Cargo.toml\");\n\n        let mut root_version = None;\n        let members: Vec<PackageMember> = metadata\n            .workspace_packages()\n            .iter()\n            .map(|pkg| {\n                let path = pkg\n                    .manifest_path\n                    .parent()\n                    .unwrap()\n                    .as_std_path()\n                    .to_path_buf();\n                let has_binary = pkg.targets.iter().any(|t| t.is_bin());\n\n                // If this package's manifest is at the workspace root, capture its version\n                if pkg.manifest_path.as_std_path() == root_manifest {\n                    root_version = Some(pkg.version.to_string());\n                }\n\n                PackageMember {\n                    name: pkg.name.clone(),\n                    version: pkg.version.to_string(),\n                    path,\n                    has_binary,\n                }\n            })\n            .collect();\n\n        Ok(WorkspaceInfo {\n            root,\n            members,\n            version: root_version,\n        })"
}

I'll add a `get_package_version` function:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01S16XKEWFDonUZ6XbNuRMGP
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/find_paths.rs",
  "old_string": "    bail!(\"could not find package name in {}\", cargo_toml.display());\n}\n\n/// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]",
  "new_string": "    bail!(\"could not find package name in {}\", cargo_toml.display());\n}\n\n/// Read the version from a Cargo.toml [package] section.\npub fn get_package_version(crate_dir: &Path) -> Result<String> {\n    let cargo_toml = crate_dir.join(\"Cargo.toml\");\n    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"failed to read {}\", cargo_toml.display()))?;\n\n    let mut in_package = false;\n    for line in content.lines() {\n        let line = line.trim();\n        if line == \"[package]\" {\n            in_package = true;\n            continue;\n        }\n        if line.starts_with('[') {\n            in_package = false;\n            continue;\n        }\n        if in_package\n            && line.starts_with(\"version\")\n            && let Some(eq_pos) = line.find('=')\n        {\n            let value = line[eq_pos + 1..].trim();\n            let value = value.trim_matches('\"').trim_matches('\\'');\n            return Ok(value.to_string());\n        }\n    }\n    bail!(\n        \"could not find package version in {}\",\n        cargo_toml.display()\n    );\n}\n\n/// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]"
}
```

## Summary: Test Fixture Exploration

### 1. **pop-ws Directory Structure**

**Root Cargo.toml** (`/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/fixtures/pop-ws/Cargo.toml`):

```

**mylib/Cargo.toml** (`/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/fixtures/pop-ws/mylib/Cargo.toml`):
```toml
[package]
name = "mylib"
version = "0.1.0"
edition = "2024"
```

**Structure:**
- **pop-ws-app**: Binary (root package with binary in `src/main.rs` + workspace definition)
- **mylib**: Library (workspace member with lib in `mylib/src/lib.rs`)
- Dependency: pop-ws-app depends on mylib

---

### 2. **popws-3p Directory Structure**

**Root Cargo.toml** (`/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/fixtures/popws-3p/Cargo.toml`):
```toml
[workspace]
members = ["app-a", "lib-b", "multi-c"]
resolver = "2"
```
(Pure workspace, no root `[package]`)

**app-a/Cargo.toml** (`/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/fixtures/popws-3p/app-a/Cargo.toml`):
```toml
[package]
name = "app-a"
version = "0.1.0"
edition = "2024"

[dependencies]
lib-b = { path = "../lib-b" }
```

**lib-b/Cargo.toml** (`/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/fixtures/popws-3p/lib-b/Cargo.toml`):
```toml
[package]
name = "lib-b"

edition = "2024"
```

**multi-c/Cargo.toml** (`/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/fixtures/popws-3p/multi-c/Cargo.toml`):
```toml
[package]
name = "multi-c"
version = "0.1.0"
edition = "2024"

[lib]
name = "multi_c"

[[bin]]
name = "mc-alpha"
path = "src/bin/mc_alpha.rs"

[[bin]]
name = "mc-beta"
path = "src/bin/mc_beta.rs"
```

**Targets Summary:**
- **app-a**: Binary (binary in `src/main.rs`), depends on lib-b
- **lib-b**: Library-only (lib in `src/lib.rs`)
- **multi-c**: Library + 2 binaries (lib in `src/lib.rs`, binaries: mc-alpha, mc-beta)

---

### 3. **Integration Tests in integration_test.rs**

**pop-ws tests** (lines 95-188):

1. `pop_ws_spec_loads_correctly()` - Loads spec and verifies panic=Abort, profile=release
2. `pop_ws_cargo_build_succeeds()` - Runs `cargo build`
3. `pop_ws_tspec_build_root_package()` - Runs `tspec build .` (builds root package)
4. `pop_ws_tspec_build_member_by_name()` - Runs `tspec build -p mylib` (builds specific member)
5. `pop_ws_tspec_build_all()` - Runs `tspec build -w` (builds workspace)
6. `pop_ws_tspec_build_from_member_dir()` - Runs `tspec build .` from `mylib/` subdir

**popws-3p tests** (lines 389-451):

1. `popws3p_build_all()` - Runs `tspec build` (all packages), verifies app-a, lib-b, multi-c in output
2. `popws3p_build_single_package()` - Runs `tspec build -p app-a` (single package)
3. `popws3p_test_all()` - Runs `tspec test`, verifies 7 passed, 0 failed, all 3 packages in output

**--manifest-path / --mp tests** (lines 457-572, using popws-3p fixture):

1. `mp_build_workspace_dir()` - Runs `tspec build --mp <workspace-dir>` from /tmp
2. `mp_build_single_package()` - Runs `tspec build --mp <dir> -p app-a` from /tmp
3. `mp_build_subpackage_dir()` - Runs `tspec build --mp app-a/` (resolves to workspace root)
4. `mp_build_cargo_toml_path()` - Runs `tspec build --mp lib-b/Cargo.toml` (resolves to workspace root)
5. `mp_test_workspace()` - Runs `tspec test --mp <dir>` (verifies 7 passed)
6. `mp_pop_fixture()` - Runs `tspec build --mp pop` (tests with pop fixture)

---

### Key Observations:

1. **pop-ws** is a hybrid workspace (has both root `[package]` and `[workspace]`) with 2 packages
2. **popws-3p** is a pure workspace (only `[workspace]`, no root package) with 3 packages
3. **pop-ws** versions: all at 0.1.0
4. **popws-3p** versions: all at 0.1.0
5. **popws-3p** is heavily tested for `--manifest-path` flag functionality
6. **multi-c** package is the most complex (library + 2 binaries) and is used to test multi-target builds
7. **All tests use fixture::copy_fixture()** to create temp copies before running tspec

count depends on project structure\n        }\n        // If cargo metadata fails (e.g., not in a Rust project), that's OK for CI\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn discover_populates_member_versions() {\n        let root = crate::find_paths::find_project_root().unwrap();\n        if let Ok(info) = WorkspaceInfo::discover(&root) {\n            for member in &info.members {\n                assert!(\n                    !member.version.is_empty(),\n                    \"member {} should have a version\",\n                    member.name\n                );\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn name_versioned_with_version() {\n        let info = WorkspaceInfo {\n            root: PathBuf::from(\"/tmp/myproject\"),\n            members: Vec::new(),\n            version: Some(\"1.2.3\".to_string()),\n        };\n        assert_eq!(info.name_versioned(), \"myproject v1.2.3\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn name_versioned_without_version() {\n        let info = WorkspaceInfo {\n            root: PathBuf::from(\"/tmp/myproject\"),\n            members: Vec::new(),\n            version: None,\n        };\n        assert_eq!(info.name_versioned(), \"myproject\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn discover_root_version_set_for_popws() {\n        // tspec itself is a POPWS — root has [package], so version should be Some\n        let root = crate::find_paths::find_project_root().unwrap();\n        if let Ok(info) = WorkspaceInfo::discover(&root) {\n            assert!(\n                info.version.is_some(),\n                \"tspec workspace root has [package], version should be Some\"\n            );\n        }\n    }"
}

Stopping. You said "stop" — do you want me to pause here and discuss, or did you want to change direction?

> DEVELOPER

should we add a version.rs that the tests and code can use to make it less brittle?

> AGENT

Not sure it'd help here. The versions we're testing are in the **fixtures**, not in tspec itself — they're static strings in test Cargo.tomls that we control (`0.3.0`, `0.2.0`, `0.4.0`, etc.). They only change when we deliberately change them.

A `version.rs` constants file would make sense if we were testing tspec's own version (which changes on every release), but for fixture versions the string literals in the assertions are clear and directly tied to the fixture files sitting right next to them.

If you're worried about drift between fixture Cargo.tomls and test assertions, we could define the fixture versions as constants in the test file — but that's just moving the string from one place to another without reducing brittleness.

Want me to just continue with inline version strings in the tests?

> DEVELOPER

Ok, for now hard code it and maybe figure something else out later.
