Perfect! Now I have all the information needed. Let me create a comprehensive summary of the findings.

## Summary: Spec Hash Display and Usage in tspec

Based on my search through the codebase, here's how spec hashes are currently displayed and used:

### 1. Hash Computation (`src/tspec.rs`)

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/tspec.rs` (lines 27-33)

```rust
/// Compute hash of a resolved spec (first 8 hex chars)
pub fn hash_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String> {
    let toml_str = serialize_spec(spec)?;
    let mut hasher = Sha256::new();
    hasher.update(toml_str.as_bytes());
    let result = hasher.finalize();
    Ok(hex::encode(&result[..4]))
}
```

The hash is computed as the **first 8 hex characters** of SHA256 (taking the first 4 bytes and encoding as hex).

### 2. Where Hashes Appear

**Snapshot Filenames** (`src/tspec.rs` lines 110-124, 129-143)
- Used in backup and snapshot filenames: `{name}-{seq:03}-{hash}{TSPEC_SUFFIX}`
- Example: `tspec.static-opt-001-a1b2c3d4.ts.toml`
- The hash is computed but NOT displayed separately to the user

### 3. "Building X with spec" Messages

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cargo_build.rs` (lines 204-212)

```rust
let verb = match mode {
    CargoMode::Build => "Building",
    CargoMode::Test => "Testing",
};

// Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo subcommand
let mut cmd = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
    let path = tspec_path.as_ref().unwrap();
    println!("{} {} with spec {}", verb, pkg_name, path.display());
```

This prints messages like:
- `"Building tspec with spec /path/to/tspec.ts.toml"`
- `"Testing tspec-build with spec /path/to/minimal.ts.toml"`

**The spec path is displayed, NOT the hash.**

### 4. Spec Column in Summary Tables

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/all.rs`

**OpResult struct** (lines 85-94):
```rust
pub struct OpResult {
    pub name: String,
    pub version: String,
    pub spec: String,  // ← stores spec label (filename only)
    pub success: bool,
    pub message: String,
    pub size: Option<u64>,
    pub test_counts: Option<TestResult>,
}
```

**spec_label function** (lines 78-83):
```rust
/// Extract a short spec label from an optional tspec path.
fn spec_label(tspec: &Option<String>) -> String {
    match tspec {
        Some(path) => spec_name_from_path(Path::new(path)),
        None => String::new(),
    }
}
```

**SummaryRow struct** (lines 355-371):
```rust
struct SummaryRow {
    name: String,
    version: String,
    spec: String,  // ← stores the label extracted by spec_label()
    detail: String,
}
```

The spec column displays the **filename without path or extension**, extracted via `spec_name_from_path()`.

**Examples from usage:**
- Lines 135, 222, 311: `let spec = spec_label(tspec);` is stored in OpResult
- The summary tables (lines 395-441) use this string to display the spec

### 5. spec_name_from_path Function

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/tspec.rs` (lines 36-47)

```rust
/// Extract spec name from path by stripping the .ts.toml (or .toml) suffix
pub fn spec_name_from_path(path: &Path) -> String {
    let filename = path
        .file_name()
        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())
        .unwrap_or_default();

    filename
        .strip_suffix(TSPEC_SUFFIX)
        .or_else(|| filename.strip_suffix(".toml"))
        .unwrap_or(&filename)
        .to_string()
}
```

**Test examples** (lines 263-278):
- `/foo/bar/tspec.static-opt.ts.toml` → `"tspec.static-opt"`
- `/foo/bar/minimal.toml` → `"minimal"`
- `/foo/bar/weird.txt` → `"weird.txt"`

### Summary Table Display Example

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/all.rs` (lines 380-441)

The `print_summary_table()` function uses a dynamic "Spec" column:
- Only shown if `has_spec == true` (i.e., any row has a non-empty spec)
- The column header is `"Spec"` and displays the result of `spec_label()` 
- Example layout:
  ```
  Package      Spec           Status    Size
  tspec v0.15.5  minimal      [ OK ]   5.2M
  tspec-build v0.15.5  static-opt [ OK ]   1.1M
  ```

---

**Key Finding:** The hash is computed and used internally for snapshot filenames, but **the user-facing display shows only the spec filename (via `spec_name_from_path()`), never the hash.**

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     2→use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→
     5→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     6→use crate::types::Spec;
     7→
     8→/// Load a spec from a TOML file
     9→pub fn load_spec(path: &Path) -> Result<Spec> {
    10→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(path)
    11→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read spec file: {}", path.display()))?;
    12→    let spec = parse_spec(&content)?;
    13→    Ok(spec)
    14→}
    15→
    16→/// Parse a spec from TOML string
    17→pub fn parse_spec(toml_str: &str) -> Result<Spec> {
    18→    toml::from_str(toml_str).context("failed to parse spec TOML")
    19→}
    20→
    21→/// Serialize a spec to TOML string (canonical form for hashing)
    22→pub fn serialize_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String> {
    23→    toml::to_string(spec).context("failed to serialize spec")
    24→}
    25→
    26→/// Compute hash of a resolved spec (first 8 hex chars)
    27→pub fn hash_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String> {
    28→    let toml_str = serialize_spec(spec)?;
    29→    let mut hasher = Sha256::new();
    30→    hasher.update(toml_str.as_bytes());
    31→    let result = hasher.finalize();
    32→    Ok(hex::encode(&result[..4]))
    33→}
    34→
    35→/// Extract spec name from path by stripping the .ts.toml (or .toml) suffix
    36→pub fn spec_name_from_path(path: &Path) -> String {
    37→    let filename = path
    38→        .file_name()
    39→        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())
    40→        .unwrap_or_default();
    41→
    42→    filename
    43→        .strip_suffix(TSPEC_SUFFIX)
    44→        .or_else(|| filename.strip_suffix(".toml"))
    45→        .unwrap_or(&filename)
    46→        .to_string()
    47→}
    48→
    49→/// Expand template placeholders in a spec's target_dir field.
    50→/// Returns None if target_dir is absent or empty.

+/// Read the version from a Cargo.toml [package] section.
+pub fn get_package_version(crate_dir: &Path) -> Result<String> {
+    let cargo_toml = crate_dir.join("Cargo.toml");
+    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)
+        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml.display()))?;
+
+    let mut in_package = false;
+    for line in content.lines() {
+        let line = line.trim();
+        if line == "[package]" {
+            in_package = true;
+            continue;
+        }
+        if line.starts_with('[') {
+            in_package = false;
+            continue;
+        }
+        if in_package
+            && line.starts_with("version")
+            && let Some(eq_pos) = line.find('=')
+        {
+            let value = line[eq_pos + 1..].trim();
+            let value = value.trim_matches('"').trim_matches('\'');
+            return Ok(value.to_string());
+        }
+    }
+    bail!("could not find package version in {}", cargo_toml.display());
+}
+
 /// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]
 /// For workspaces, returns the directory containing the workspace Cargo.toml
 /// For POPs (Plain Old Packages), returns the directory containing the package Cargo.toml

+name = "my-test-crate"
+version = "1.2.3"
+"#,
+        )
+        .unwrap();
+
+        let ver = get_package_version(tmp.path()).unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(ver, "1.2.3");
+    }
+

+        let cargo_toml = tmp.path().join("Cargo.toml");

+
+        let result = get_package_version(tmp.path());
+        assert!(result.is_err());

+                .unwrap_err()

+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+// Version in SUMMARY tests
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+#[test]
+fn pop_ws_build_summary_shows_versions() {
+    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture("pop-ws");
+
+    let output = Command::new(tspec_bin())
+        .args(["build", "-w"])
+        .current_dir(&project)
+        .output()
+        .expect("failed to run tspec build -w");
+
+    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
+    assert!(
+        output.status.success(),
+        "tspec build -w failed:\n{}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
+    );
+    // Header should include root package version
+    assert!(
+        stdout.contains("pop-ws v0.3.0 BUILD SUMMARY"),
+        "missing versioned header in:\n{stdout}"
+    );
+    // Rows should show per-package versions
+    assert!(
+        stdout.contains("pop-ws-app v0.3.0"),
+        "missing pop-ws-app version in:\n{stdout}"
+    );
+    assert!(
+        stdout.contains("mylib v0.2.0"),
+        "missing mylib version in:\n{stdout}"
+    );
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn popws3p_test_summary_shows_versions() {
+    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture("popws-3p");
+
+    let output = Command::new(tspec_bin())
+        .args(["test"])
+        .current_dir(&project)
+        .output()
+        .expect("failed to run tspec test");
+
+    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
+    assert!(
+        output.status.success(),
+        "tspec test failed:\n{}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
+    );
+    // Virtual workspace — header should NOT have a version
+    assert!(
+        stdout.contains("popws-3p TEST SUMMARY"),
+        "missing header in:\n{stdout}"
+    );
+    // Rows should show per-package versions
+    assert!(
+        stdout.contains("app-a v0.4.0"),
+        "missing app-a version in:\n{stdout}"
+    );
+    assert!(
+        stdout.contains("lib-b v0.5.0"),
+        "missing lib-b version in:\n{stdout}"
+    );
+    assert!(
+        stdout.contains("multi-c v0.6.0"),
+        "missing multi-c version in:\n{stdout}"
+    );
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn pop_ws_run_summary_shows_versions() {
+    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture("pop-ws");
+
+    let output = Command::new(tspec_bin())
+        .args(["run", "-w"])
+        .current_dir(&project)
+        .output()
+        .expect("failed to run tspec run -w");
+
+    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
+    assert!(
+        output.status.success(),
+        "tspec run -w failed:\n{}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
+    );
+    assert!(
+        stdout.contains("pop-ws v0.3.0 RUN SUMMARY"),
+        "missing versioned header in:\n{stdout}"
+    );
+    assert!(
+        stdout.contains("pop-ws-app v0.3.0"),
+        "missing pop-ws-app version in:\n{stdout}"
+    );
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn pop_ws_compare_summary_shows_versions() {
+    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture("pop-ws");
+
+    let output = Command::new(tspec_bin())
+        .args(["compare", "-w"])
+        .current_dir(&project)
+        .output()
+        .expect("failed to run tspec compare -w");
+
+    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
+    assert!(
+        output.status.success(),
+        "tspec compare -w failed:\n{}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
+    );
+    // Single binary package — should show per-package compare header with version
+    assert!(
+        stdout.contains("pop-ws-app v0.3.0 COMPARE SUMMARY"),
+        "missing versioned compare header in:\n{stdout}"
+    );
+}
